# Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin

ברטנורא על משנה סנהדרין

Link: [https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin)

חיבור התשתית הקדום של חז״ל, המתעד מגוון דעות הלכתיות ממסורת התורה שבעל פה. נחתם בשנת 200 לספירה לערך.

16th-century commentary printed in most editions of the Mishnah, including summaries of talmudic discussions and legal conclusions.

Sections: Chapter-Mishnah-Comment

Location: Mishnah/Rishonim on Mishnah/Bartenura/Seder Nezikin

## Chapter 1


### Mishnah 1

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 1:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:1:1)


**דִּינֵי מָמוֹנוֹת.** דְּהַיְנוּ הוֹדָאוֹת וְהַלְוָאוֹת:


דיני ממונות – that is admissions (according to Tractate Shevuot 6:1 – worth at least one Perutah) and loans.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 1:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:1:2)


**בִּשְׁלשָׁה.** הֶדְיוֹטוֹת. וְלֹא אַצְרְכוּהוּ רַבָּנָן לִשְׁלֹשָׁה מֻמְחִין, שֶׁלֹּא תִנְעֹל דֶּלֶת בִּפְנֵי לֹוִין, שֶׁמָּא יִכְפֹּר הַלֹּוֶה וְלֹא יִמְצָא מֻמְחִין לְכוֹפוֹ לַדִּין אֶלָּא יָחִיד מֻמְחֶה אוֹ שְׁלֹשָׁה הֶדְיוֹטוֹת. אֲבָל גְּזֵלוֹת וַחֲבָלוֹת בִּשְׁלשָׁה מֻמְחִין, דֵּאלֹהִים כְּתִיב בְּפָרָשַׁת שׁוֹמְרִים בְּוְאֵלֶּה הַמִּשְׁפָּטִים שָׁלשׁ פְּעָמִים. וּמִנַּהּ יָלְפִינַן שְׁלֹשָׁה מֻמְחִין:


בשלשה – commoners/lay persons, for the Rabbis did not require three specialists, in order not to lock the door before loans, lest the borrower confess and specialists are not found to force him to court, but rather, or one [judge] would be a specialist or [all] three are commoners; but cases of theft and mayhem [require] three specialists, for [the word] אלהים/”God” (which means judges) is written in the section of the guardians in [the Torah portion] of “And these are the statutes” (Exodus, chapter 21 and beyond; specifically, the verses are: Exodus 22:6-8) , and from it we derive three specialists.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 1:1:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:1:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:1:3)


**נֶזֶק.** אָדָם אוֹ שׁוֹר הַמּוּעָד שֶׁהִזִּיקוּ, דִּמְשַׁלְּמִים נֶזֶק שָׁלֵם:


נזק – [damage caused] by man or by a warned bull , who damaged him for which they pay complete damages.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 1:1:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:1:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:1:4)


**חֲצִי נֶזֶק.** שׁוֹר תָּם שֶׁהִזִּיק. וְאַף עַל גַּב דְּהַיְנוּ חֲבָלוֹת, אַיְדֵי דְבָעֵי לְמִתְנֵי תַּשְׁלוּמֵי כֶפֶל וְתַשְׁלוּמֵי אַרְבָּעָה וַחֲמִשָּׁה שֶׁאֵינוֹ מְשַׁלֵּם כְּמוֹ מַה שֶּׁהִזִּיק, שֶׁמְּשַׁלֵּם יוֹתֵר, תָּנָא נַמִּי חֲצִי נֶזֶק שֶׁאֵינוֹ מְשַׁלֵּם כְּמוֹ שֶׁהִזִּיק, שֶׁהֲרֵי מְשַׁלֵּם פָּחוֹת. וְאַיְדֵי דְתָנָא חֲצִי נֶזֶק תָּנָא נַמִּי נֶזֶק:


חצי נזק – an innocuous bull, who caused damage (and even though this is mayhem) , since it was necessary to teach in the Mishnah “double fines, and four and five-times payment, which he doesn’t pay like what he damages, when he pays more. The Tanna [our Mishnah] also taught half-damage in which he does not pay what he damaged, for he pays less. And since the Mishnah taught half-damage, it also taught [full] damages.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 1:1:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:1:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:1:5)


**וְהַמּוֹצִיא שֵׁם רַע.** לֹא מָצָאתִי לְבִתְּךָ בְּתוּלִים וְעָנְשׁוּ אֹתוֹ מֵאָה כֶסֶף:


ומוציא שם רע – “I did not find your daughter a virgin” (Deuteronomy 22:17); “and they shall fine him one hundred shekels of silver [and give it to the girl’s father for the man has defamed a virgin in Israel…]” (Deuteronomy 22:19).


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 1:1:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:1:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:1:6)


**דִּינֵי נְפָשׁוֹת.** דְּאִם אֱמֶת הָיָה הַדָּבָר וְזִנְּתָה תַחְתָּיו וּסְקָלוּהָ. וְדִינֵי נְפָשׁוֹת בְּעֶשְׂרִים וּשְׁלשָׁה כְּדִלְקַמָּן. וַהֲלָכָה כַּחֲכָמִים:


דיני נפשות – and if the matter was found to be true that she was unfaithful [to her husband] and they stoned her. And capital cases [are judged] by twenty-three, as seen further, and Halakha is according to the Sages.



### Mishnah 2

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 1:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:2:1)


**מַכּוֹת.** מַלְקוֹת אַרְבָּעִים:


מכות – forty stripes


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 1:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:2:2)


**בִּשְׁלֹשָׁה.** דִּכְתִיב (דברים כה) וְנִגְּשׁוּ אֶל הַמִּשְׁפָּט וּשְׁפָטוּם, שְׁנַיִם, וְאֵין בֵּית דִּין שָׁקוּל מוֹסִיפִין עֲלֵיהֶם עוֹד אֶחָד, הֲרֵי כָאן שְׁלֹשָׁה:


בשלשה – as it is written (Deuteronomy 25:1): “…and they go to law, and a decision is rendered.” The word ושפטום/and they go to law – [denotes] two, and there can never be an even-balanced court, so we add upon them another [judge], for we have three [judges].


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 1:2:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:2:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:2:3)


**רַבִּי יִשְׁמָעֵאל אוֹמֵר בְּעֶשְׂרִים וּשְׁלשָׁה.** אַתְיָא רָשָׁע רָשָׁע, כְּתִיב הָכָא (שם) וְהִרְשִׁיעוּ אֶת הָרָשָׁע, וּכְתִיב הָתָם (במדבר לה) אֲשֶׁר הוּא רָשָׁע לָמוּת. מַה לְהַלָּן בְּעֶשְׂרִים וּשְׁלשָׁה, אַף כָּאן בְּעֶשְׂרִים וּשְׁלֹשָׁה:


רבי ישמעאל אומר: בעשרים ושלשה – it comes [by a comparison of the word] רשע רשע –[found in two different places by analogy]. It is written here (Deuteronomy 25:1) “declared the one in the right and the other in the wrong”, and it is written there (Numbers 35:31): “[You may not accept a ransom for the life of a murderer] who is guilty of capital crime; [he must be put to death].”Just as there (i.e., in Numbers 35:31), with twenty-three [judges], so here (Deuteronomy 25:1) with twenty-three [judges].


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 1:2:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:2:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:2:4)


**עִבּוּר הַחֹדֶשׁ.** קִדּוּשׁ הַחֹדֶשׁ. וְאַיְדֵי דְבָעֵי לְמִתְנֵי עִבּוּר שָׁנָה תָּנָא נַמִּי עִבּוּר הַחֹדֶשׁ:


עיבור החודש – the Sanctification of the [New] Month, and since it was necessary [for the Mishnah] to teach the intercalation of the year (i.e., proclaiming a leap-year/inserting a second Adar), so too it taught the intercalation of a month (i.e., proclaiming a month just past one of thirty days).


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 1:2:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:2:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:2:5)


**בִּשְׁלשָׁה מַתְחִילִין.** לִרְאוֹת אִם יֵשׁ לְהוֹשִׁיב בֵּית דִּין עַל כָּךְ. אִם אֶחָד מֵהַשְּׁלשָׁה אוֹמֵר צָרִיךְ בֵּית דִּין לֵישֵׁב וּלְעַיֵּן אִם צְרִיכָה שָׁנָה לְעַבֵּר, מִפְּנֵי הַתְּקוּפָה וְהָאָבִיב וְהַפֵּרוֹת, וּשְׁנַיִם אוֹמְרִים אֵין צָרִיךְ, שֶׁאֵין כָּאן סָפֵק, דְּוַדַּאי אֵינָהּ צְרִיכָה עִבּוּר, בָּטֵל יָחִיד בְּמִעוּטוֹ. שְׁנַיִם אוֹמְרִים לֵישֵׁב וְאֶחָד אוֹמֵר שֶׁלֹּא לֵישֵׁב, הוֹלְכִין אַחַר הַשְּׁנַיִם, וּמוֹסִיפִין עוֹד שְׁנַיִם אֲחֵרִים שֶׁיִּשְׂאוּ וְיִתְּנוּ בַדָּבָר, וַהֲרֵי כָאן חֲמִשָּׁה. שְׁנַיִם אוֹמְרִים צָרִיךְ לְעַבֵּר וּשְׁלשָׁה אוֹמְרִים אֵין צָרִיךְ, בָּטְלוּ שְׁנַיִם בְּמִעוּטָן. שְׁנַיִם אוֹמְרִים אֵינָהּ צְרִיכָה וּשְׁלשָׁה אוֹמְרִים צְרִיכָה, הוֹלְכִים אַחַר הָרֹב, וּמוֹסִיפִין עוֹד שְׁנַיִם שֶׁיִּהְיוּ שִׁבְעָה וּמְעַבְּרִין אוֹתָהּ. וּבַגְּמָרָא מְפָרֵשׁ דְּהָנֵי שְׁלֹשָׁה חֲמִשָּׁה וְשִׁבְעָה כְּנֶגֶד בִּרְכַּת כֹּהֲנִים, שֶׁיֵּשׁ בְּפָסוּק רִאשׁוֹן שָׁלשׁ תֵּבוֹת, וּבַשֵּׁנִי חָמֵשׁ, וּבַשְּׁלִישִׁי שֶׁבַע. וַהֲלָכָה כְּרַבָּן שִׁמְעוֹן בֶּן גַּמְלִיאֵל:


בשלשה מתחילין – to see if one is able to appoint a Jewish court on this. And if one of the three [judges] says that there is a need for the Jewish court to sit and investigate if it is necessary to intercalate the year because of the solstice, and the spring and the fruition, but two say that it is not necessary, for there is no doubt here and for sure it is not necessary to have a leap year, the individual is nullified in his minority [status]. Two [judges] who say to appoint a Jewish court and one [judge] says not to appoint [a Jewish court], we follow after the two [judges], and we add two other [judges] who will engage in give-and-take in the matter, and hence we have here five. If two [judges] say that we must intercalate [the year] and three [judges] say that it is not necessary, the two are nullified in their minority. [But if] two [judges] say that it is not necessary and three [judges] say that it is necessary, we follow after the majority, and add another two [judges] so that that there will be seven and we intercalate it [i.e., the year]. And the Gemara explains that these three, five and seven correspond to the Priestly Blessing, for there are three words in the first verse, and in the second, five [words] and in the third [verse] seven [words], and the Halakah is according to Rabban Shimon ben Gamaliel.



### Mishnah 3

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 1:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:3:1)


**סְמִיכַת זְקֵנִים.** עַל רֹאשׁ פַּר הֶעְלֵם דָּבָר שֶׁל צִבּוּר. וְיֵשׁ בְּמַשְׁמָעוּת הַדְּבָרִים גַּם מִנּוּי הַדַּיָּן, שֶׁהַגָּדוֹל הַסּוֹמֵךְ צָרִיךְ שֶׁיְּצָרֵף עִמּוֹ שְׁנַיִם כְּשֶׁרוֹצֶה לִסְמֹךְ חָכָם כְּדֵי שֶׁיִּקָּרֵא רַבִּי וְיָדוּן דִּינֵי קְנָסוֹת. וּלְשׁוֹן סְמִיכָה, מִשּׁוּם דִּכְתִיב (במדבר כז) וַיִּסְמֹךְ אֶת יָדָיו עָלָיו. וְלָאו דְּבָעֵי מִסְמָךְ יְדֵיהּ עֲלֵיהּ, אֶלָּא בִשְׁמָא קָרוּ לֵיהּ רַבִּי. וְאֵין סְמִיכָה בְחוּצָה לָאָרֶץ, אֶלָּא צָרִיךְ שֶׁהַסּוֹמֵךְ וְהַנִּסְמָךְ יִהְיוּ כֻלָּן בְּאֶרֶץ יִשְׂרָאֵל, וְאָז יִהְיֶה לוֹ רְשׁוּת לָדוּן דִּינֵי קְנָסוֹת וַאֲפִלּוּ בְחוּץ לָאָרֶץ, לְפִי שֶׁסַּנְהֶדְרִין נוֹהֶגֶת בֵּין בָּאָרֶץ בֵּין בְּחוּץ לָאָרֶץ, אַחַר שֶׁנִּסְמְכוּ בָאָרֶץ. וְכָתַב הָרַמְבַּ"ם שֶׁנִּרְאֶה לוֹ שֶׁבַּזְּמַן הַזֶּה שֶׁאֵין לָנוּ סָמוּךְ אִישׁ מִפִּי אִישׁ עַד מֹשֶׁה רַבֵּנוּ, אִם הָיוּ מַסְכִּימִים כָּל הַחֲכָמִים שֶׁבְּאֶרֶץ יִשְׂרָאֵל לִסְמֹךְ יָחִיד אוֹ רַבִּים, הֲרֵי אֵלּוּ סְמוּכִים וִיכוֹלִים לָדוּן דִּינֵי קְנָסוֹת וְיֵשׁ לָהֶן לִסְמֹךְ לַאֲחֵרִים. וְהַדָּבָר צָרִיךְ הֶכְרֵעַ:


סמיכת זקנים – on the head of a bullock over a matter that escaped the notice of the community. And there is in the implication of these things also the appointment of a judge, for the greatest one who is ordained, needs to combine with him two [others] when he wants to ordain a Sage in order in order that he will be called “Rabbi” and will adjudicate laws of fines. And we use the language of סמיכה / laying of hands/ordination because it is written (Numbers 27:23): “He laid his hands upon him [and commissioned him – as the LORD had spoken through Moses].” And not that one requires the ordaining with his hands upon him, but by name, as they call him “Rabbi.” But there is no ordination outside of the Land of Israel, but it is necessary that the one who ordains and the ordainee are all together in the Land of Israel, for then he will have permission to adjudicate the laws of fines, and even outside of the Land, for the [authority of the] Sanhedrin applies both in the Land [of Israel] and outside of the Land [of Israel], after they have been ordained in the Land [of Israel]. And Maimonides wrote that it appeared to him that since we no longer have men ordained by the mouth of other men [going back] until the time of Moses our Teacher, if all the Sages in the Land of Israel would each ordain one individual or many, it would be that they would be ordained and able to adjudicate the laws of fines and they would be able to ordain others. And this matter requires a verdict by a majority of one.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 1:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:3:2)


**וַעֲרִיפַת הָעֶגְלָה בִּשְׁלשָׁה.** דִּכְתִיב (ויקרא ד) זִקְנֵי הָעֵדָה, מִעוּט זִקְנֵי שְׁנַיִם, וְאֵין בֵּית דִּין שָׁקוּל, הוֹסֵיף עֲלֵיהֶם עוֹד אֶחָד הֲרֵי שְׁלשָׁה:


ועריפת העגלה בשלשה – since it is written (Leviticus 4:15): “ The elders of the community [lay their hands upon the head of the bull before the LORD…”, the lowest [value] of [the word] זקנים/elders is two, and there cannot be an even-balanced court, they add upon them another “one,” so that there are three.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 1:3:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:3:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:3:3)


**וְרַבִּי יְהוּדָה אוֹמֵר בַּחֲמִשָּׁה.** וְסָמְכוּ, שְׁנַיִם. זִקְנֵי, שְׁנַיִם. וְאֵין בֵּית דִּין שָׁקוּל, הוֹסֵיף עֲלֵיהֶם עוֹד אֶחָד, הֲרֵי חֲמִשָּׁה. וַהֲלָכָה כְּרַבִּי יְהוּדָה:


רבי יהודה אומר: בחמשה – [The word] וסמכו/and they lay their hands [denotes] “two”, [and the word] זקני/elders [denotes] two, and there cannot be an even-balanced court, they add upon them another “one,” so that there are five, and the Halakha is according to Rabbi Yehuda.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 1:3:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:3:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:3:4)


**מִצְוַת חֲלִיצָה בִּשְׁלשָׁה.** דִּכְתִיב (דברים כה) וְנִגְּשָׁה יְבִמְתּוֹ אֵלָיו לְעֵינֵי הַזְּקֵנִים, זְקֵנִים, שְׁנַיִם. וְאֵין בֵּית דִּין שָׁקוּל, הוֹסֵיף עֲלֵיהֶם עוֹד אֶחָד, הֲרֵי שְׁלשָׁה. וּשְׁנַיִם אֲחֵרִים שֶׁמּוֹסִיפִים אֵינוֹ אֶלָּא לְפִרְסוּמֵי מִלְּתָא:


מצות חליצה בשלשה – As it is written (Deuteronomy 25:9): “the brother’s widow shall go up to him in the presence of the elders…” The word זקנים /elders [denotes] two , and there cannot be an even-handed court, they add upon them another “one,” which makes three. And the other two which they add on are not [added] other than to publicize the matter.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 1:3:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:3:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:3:5)


**מֵאוּנִין.** קְטַנָּה יְתוֹמָה שֶׁהִשִּׂיאוּהָ אִמָּהּ וְאַחֶיהָ לְדַעְתָּהּ וְיוֹצְאָה בְמֵאוּן שֶׁמְּמָאֶנֶת בְּבַעְלָהּ, צָרִיךְ שֶׁיִּהְיֶה בִשְׁלשָׁה. וּבִיבָמוֹת מוּכָח דְּמֵאוּן בִּפְנֵי שְׁנַיִם מַסְפִּיק:


מיאונין – A minor orphan girl whose mother or brothers married her off, with her knowledge, and may go free with her protest against a marriage to her husband [contracted during her minority], which must take place [with a Jewish court] of three [judges], and in the Tractate Yevamot, it is proved that a woman’s protest it is enough [that the protest is lodged] in front of two [judges].


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 1:3:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:3:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:3:6)


**נֶטַע רְבָעִי.** אִם בָּא לְחַלְּלוֹ עַל הַמָּעוֹת, וְכֵן מַעֲשֵׂר שֵׁנִי, וְאֵין דְּמֵיהֶן יְדוּעִים. כְּגוֹן פֵּרוֹת וְהִרְקִיבוּ שֶׁאֵין לָהֶם שַׁעַר יָדוּעַ:


נטע רבעי – if he comes to redeem [them] by setting aside its monetary value, and similarly for the Second Tithe, where their value is not known, such as fruits that rotted and they do not have a known market rate.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 1:3:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:3:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:3:7)


**הַהֶקְדֵּשׁוֹת.** הַבָּא לִפְדּוֹתָן צָרִיךְ שְׁלֹשָׁה לְשׁוּמָן:


וההקדשות – One who comes to redeem them requires three [judges] to estimate [their value].


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 1:3:8

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:3:8](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:3:8)


**הָעֲרָכִין הַמִּטַּלְטְלִין.** הֲרֵי שֶׁאָמַר עֵרֶךְ פְּלוֹנִי עָלַי, וְאֵין לוֹ מָעוֹת לִתֵּן כְּפִי הַדָּמִים הַקְּצוּבִים בַּפָּרָשָׁה, וּבָא לִתֵּן מִטַּלְטְלִין, צָרִיךְ שְׁלֹשָׁה לָשׁוּם אוֹתָן מִטַּלְטְלִים:


הערכין המיטלטלין – For a person who said: “[I vow] the value of so-and-so upon myself”, and he lacks money to give according to the established price and he comes to give [the equivalent] in movable objects, requires three [judges] to estimate [the value] of those movables.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 1:3:9

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:3:9](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:3:9)


**אֶחָד מֵהֶן כֹּהֵן.** דְּבַעֲרָכִין כֹּהֵן כְּתִיב (ויקרא כז) כְּעֶרְכְּךָ הַכֹּהֵן:


אחד מהן כהן – that in valuations [of a person or animal dedicated to the Temple], a Kohen is written (Leviticus 27:12): “whatever assessment is set by the priest shall stand.”


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 1:3:10

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:3:10](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:3:10)


**וְהַקַּרְקָעוֹת.** וְאִם אֵין לוֹ מִטַּלְטְלִין וּבָא לִתֵּן קַרְקַע, צָרִיךְ שֶׁיָּשׁוּמוּ עֲשָׂרָה אֲנָשִׁים וְאֶחָד מֵהֶן כֹּהֵן אוֹתָהּ קַרְקַע שֶׁיִּהְיֶה כְּפִי הָעֵרֶךְ שֶׁיֵּשׁ עָלָיו לִתֵּן:


והקרקעות – and if he lacks movable [objects] and comes to give land, it requires ten individuals who will assess [the value of] the land, and one of them must be a Kohen, that it will be according the value that he must give.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 1:3:11

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:3:11](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:3:11)


**וְאָדָם כַּיּוֹצֵא בָהֶן.** וְאִם אָמַר דְּמֵי פְלוֹנִי עָלַי, שֶׁשָּׁמִין אוֹתוֹ כַּמָּה הוּא יָפֶה לִמָּכֵר בַּשּׁוּק וְנוֹתֵן דָּמָיו, צָרִיךְ גַּם כֵּן שֶׁיִּהְיוּ עֲשָׂרָה וְאֶחָד מֵהֶן כֹּהֵן בְּשׁוּמָא זוֹ:


ואדם כיוצא בהן – and if he said, the value of so-and-so is upon me, that we appraise the how much worth he would have to be sold in the marketplace, and he gives its monetary value, and this also requires that there would be ten [individuals], and one of them a Kohen in this appraisal.



### Mishnah 4

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 1:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:4:1)


**וְהָרַגְתָּ אֶת הָאִשָּׁה וְאֶת הַבְּהֵמָה.** הַיְנוּ רוֹבֵעַ. וְאִתַּקַּשׁ לְאִשָּׁה, מָה אִשָּׁה בְּעֶשְׂרִים וּשְׁלשָׁה אַף בְּהֵמָה בְּעֶשְׂרִים וּשְׁלֹשָׁה:


והרגת את האשה ואת הבהמה – that is an animal that copulates with a woman, which is compared by analogy to a woman. Just as a woman [is tried by a court] of twenty three, so is the animal [tried by a court of twenty-three.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 1:4:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:4:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:4:2)


**וְאוֹמֵר וְאֶת הַבְּהֵמָה תַּהֲרֹגוּ.** בְּנִרְבָּע כְּתִיב. לָמַדְנוּ רוֹבֵעַ וְנִרְבָּע:


ואומר: "ואת הבהמה תהרוגו" – It is written concerning an animal that was copulated by a man. We have [thereby] learned/derived the animal that copulated with a woman and the animal that was copulated by a man.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 1:4:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:4:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:4:3)


**כְּמִיתַת הַבְּעָלִים.** כְּלוֹמַר כְּמוֹ שֶׁהָיָה הַבַּעַל נִדּוֹן בְּעֶשְׂרִים וּשְׁלֹשָׁה, אִם הָיָה חַיָּב מִיתָה:


כמיתת הבעלים – that is to say, just as the owner is judged by [a court of] twenty-three [judges], if he was liable for the death penalty.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 1:4:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:4:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:4:4)


**כָּל הַקּוֹדֵם לְהָרְגָן זָכָה.** כְּשֶׁהֵמִיתוּ אֶת הָאָדָם. וְאֵין צָרִיךְ לַהֲבִיאָם לְבֵית דִּין:


כל הקודם להורגן זכה – when they killed a human being, and there is no need to bring them to be tried in a Jewish court.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 1:4:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:4:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:4:5)


**רַבִּי עֲקִיבָא אוֹמֵר וְכוּ'.** בַּגְּמָרָא מְפָרֵשׁ דְּאִיכָּא בֵּין תַּנָּא קַמָּא לְרַבִּי עֲקִיבָא, נָחָשׁ. דִּלְתַנָּא קַמָּא נָחָשׁ מִיתָתוֹ בְּעֶשְׂרִים וּשְׁלֹשָׁה, וּלְרַבִּי עֲקִיבָא הַזְּאֵב וְהָאֲרִי וְהַדֹּב וְהַנָּמֵר וְהַבַּרְדְּלָס מִיתָתָן בְּעֶשְׂרִים וּשְׁלֹשָׁה, אֲבָל נָחָשׁ, הַקּוֹדֵם לְהָרְגוֹ זָכָה, שֶׁסִּלֵּק הַמַּזִּיק מִן הָעוֹלָם. וַהֲלָכָה כְּרַבִּי עֲקִיבָא:


רבי עקיבא אומר: וכו' – It explains in the Gemara that there is a difference between the first teacher [of the Mishnah] and Rabbi Akiva. [Concerning] a snake – where the first Tanna [of the Mishnah] claims that its death is via a Jewish court of twenty-three [judges], but according to Rabbi Akiva, [the wolf], the lion, and the bear and the leopard and the hyena , their deaths [are administered] through [a Jewish court] of twenty-three [judges], but a snake, all who can kill it earliest, is [found] worthy, for it removes the one who causes damage from the world. And the Halakha is according to Rabbi Akiva.



### Mishnah 5

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 1:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:5:1)


**אֶת הַשֵּׁבֶט.** רֻבּוֹ שֶׁל שֵׁבֶט שֶׁעָבְדוּ עֲבוֹדָה זָרָה בְּמֵזִיד, אֵין דָּנִין אֶלָּא בְּבֵית דִּין שֶׁל שִׁבְעִים וְאֶחָד. דִּכְתִיב (דברים יז) וְהוֹצֵאתָ אֶת הָאִישׁ הַהוּא אוֹ אֶת הָאִשָּׁה הַהִיא אֶל שְׁעָרֶיךָ, אִישׁ וְאִשָּׁה אַתָּה מוֹצִיא אֶל שְׁעָרֶיךָ, וְאִי אַתָּה מוֹצִיא אֶת הַשֵּׁבֶט אֶל שְׁעָרֶיךָ, אֶלָּא בְּבֵית דִּין הַגָּדוֹל:


את השבט – the majority of a tribe that intentionally worshiped idolatry, they are not judged other than with a Jewish court of seventy one, as it is written (Deuteronomy 17:5): “You shall take the man or the woman [who did that wicked thing] out to the public place….”, You can take a man or a woman out to the public place, but you cannot take out a tribe to the public place (i.e., literally, “your gates”) other than with the large Jewish court [of seventy-one members].


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 1:5:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:5:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:5:2)


**וְלֹא אֶת נְבִיא הַשֶּׁקֶר.** גָּמַר דָּבָר דָּבָר מִזָּקֵן מַמְרֵא, כְּתִיב הָכָא (שם יח) אֲשֶׁר יָזִיד לְדַבֵּר דָּבָר, וּכְתִיב בְּזָקֵן מַמְרֵא (שם יז) כִּי יִפָּלֵא מִמְּךָ דָּבָר, מַה זָּקֵן מַמְרֵא בְּבֵית דִּין הַגָּדוֹל דִּכְתִיב (שם) וְקַמְתָּ וְעָלִיתָ, אַף נְבִיא שֶׁקֶר בְּבֵית דִּין הַגָּדוֹל:


ולא את נביא השקר – The Rabbis learn/derive [by analogy from the double usage of the word] דבר דבר [from the case of] the rebellious elder [disregarding the decision of the Supreme Court]. It is written here (Deuteronomy 18:20): “[But any prophet] who presumes to speak [in My name an oracle that I did not command him to utter…],” and it is written regarding the rebellious elder (Deuteronomy 17:8): “If a case is too baffling for you to decide…” Just as the rebellious elder [is judged] in the Great Jewish court, as it is written, (Deuteronomy 17:8): “you shall promptly repair [to the place the LORD your God will have chosen], so also the false prophet [is to be tried] in the Great Jewish court.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 1:5:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:5:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:5:3)


**וְלֹא אֶת כֹּהֵן גָּדוֹל.** דְּאָמַר קְרָא (שמות יח) כָּל הַדָּבָר הַגָּדֹל יָבִיאוּ אֵלֶיךָ, דְּבָרָיו שֶׁל גָּדוֹל יָבִיאוּ אֵלֶיךָ. וּמֹשֶׁה בִּמְקוֹם שִׁבְעִים וְאֶחָד קָאֵי:


ולא את כהן גדול – For the Biblical verse states (Exodus 18:22): “…Have them bring every major dispute to you, [but let them decide every minor dispute themselves].” The matters for a great person, they should bring to you, and Moses was in place of [the court] of seventy-one.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 1:5:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:5:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:5:4)


**וְאֵין מוֹצִיאִין לְמִלְחֶמֶת הָרְשׁוּת.** כָּל מִלְחָמָה חוּץ מִמִּלְחֶמֶת שִׁבְעָה עֲמָמִין וּמִלְחֶמֶת עֲמָלֵק, קְרוּיָה מִלְחֶמֶת הָרְשׁוּת:


ואין מוציאין למלחמת הרשות – Every war except for the wars [against] the seven {Canaanite] natiosn and the war [against] Amalek, is called the optional (secular/political) war.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 1:5:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:5:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:5:5)


**אֶלָּא בְבֵית דִּין שֶׁל שִׁבְעִים וְאֶחָד.** דִּכְתִיב בְּדָוִד (דברי הימים א כז) וְאַחֲרֵי אֲחִיתֹפֶל בְּנָיָהוּ בֶן יְהוֹיָדָע. אֲחִיתֹפֶל זֶה יוֹעֵץ, בְּנָיָהוּ בֶן יְהוֹיָדָע זוֹ סַנְהֶדְרִין, שֶׁהָיָה מֻפְלָא שֶׁבְּכֻלָּן וְכֻלָּן נִגְרָרִים אַחֲרָיו:


אלא בבית דין של שבעים ואחד – as it is written (I Chronicles 27:34): “After Ahitophel were Jehoiada son of Benaiah [and Abiathar. The commander of the king’s army was Joab.” (In verse 33 is written:) “Ahitophel was a counselor to the king.” Jehoiada son of Benaiah is the Sanhedrin, for he was the greatest of them all and all of them were dragged after him. (The Bartenura or the Gemara – Sanhedrin 16b and see the Tosafot misquoted the verse).


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 1:5:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:5:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:5:6)


**עַל הָעִיר.** יְרוּשָׁלַיִם. שֶׁקְּדֻשָּׁתָהּ גְּדוֹלָה מִשְּׁאָר קְדֻשַּׁת אֶרֶץ יִשְׂרָאֵל:


על העיר – Jerusalem, whose holiness is greater than the rest of the Land of Israel (see Mishnah Kelim, Chapter 1, Mishnah 6 and following).


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 1:5:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:5:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:5:7)


**וְהָעֲזָרוֹת.** קְדֻשָּׁתָן גְּדוֹלָה מִקְּדֻשַּׁת יְרוּשָׁלָיִם. וְאִי אֶפְשָׁר לְחַדֵּשׁ קְדֻשָּׁה אֶלָּא בְּבֵית דִּין שֶׁל שִׁבְעִים וְאֶחָד, דְּאָמַר קְרָא (שמות כה) כְּכֹל אֲשֶׁר אֲנִי מַרְאֶה אוֹתְךָ וְגוֹ'‏ וְכֵן תַּעֲשׂוּ, לְדוֹרוֹת. מַה מִּשְׁכָּן קָדוֹשׁ עַל פִּי מֹשֶׁה שֶׁהוּא בִמְקוֹם סַנְהֶדְרִי גְדוֹלָה, אַף לְתוֹסֶפֶת עִיר וַעֲזָרוֹת עַל פִּי סַנְהֶדְרִי גְדוֹלָה:


והעזרות – their holiness is greater than the holiness of Jerusalem and it is impossible to renew sanctity other than in the Jewish court of seventy-one, as it states in the Biblical verse (Exodus 25:9): “Exactly as I show you, etc. – so you shall make it,” for all generations. Just as the Tabernacle is holy through the mouth of Moses who is in place of the Great Sanhedrin, even for all generations, the addition of the city [of Jerusalem] and the courtyards – by the mouth of the Great Sanhedrin.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 1:5:8

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:5:8](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:5:8)


**וְאֵין עוֹשִׂין סַנְהֶדְרָאוֹת לַשְּׁבָטִים כוּ'.** כִּדְאַשְׁכְּחַן בְּמֹשֶׁה דְּאוֹקֵי סַנְהֶדְרָאוֹת, וּמֹשֶׁה בִּמְקוֹם שִׁבְעִים וְאֶחָד קָאֵי:


ואין עושין סנהדראות לשבטים כו' – as we find with Moses who established Sanhedrins, for Moses stands in place of [the Great Sanhedrin] of seventy-one.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 1:5:9

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:5:9](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:5:9)


**אֵין עוֹשִׂין עִיר הַנִּדַּחַת כוּ'.** דִּכְתִיב (דברים יז) וְהוֹצֵאתָ אֶת הָאִישׁ הַהוּא אוֹ אֶת הָאִשָּׁה הַהִיא, אִישׁ וְאִשָּׁה אַתָּה מוֹצִיא אֶל שְׁעָרֶיךָ, לְבֵית דִּין שֶׁבְּעִירְךָ, וְאִי אַתָּה מוֹצִיא אֶת כָּל הָעִיר אֶל שְׁעָרֶיךָ, אֶלָּא לַשַּׁעַר הַמְיֻחָד:


ואין עושין עיר הנדחת – As it is written (Deuteronomy 17:5): “You shall take the man or the woman.” A man or a woman you take out to your gates (i.e., “the public place”), to the Jewish court that is in your city, but you do not take the entire city to your gates (i.e., “the public place”) other than to a special gate.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 1:5:10

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:5:10](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:5:10)


**בַּסְּפָר.** עִיר הַמַּבְדֶּלֶת בֵּין אֶרֶץ יִשְׂרָאֵל לְאֶרֶץ הָעַמִּים, דִּכְתִיב מִקִּרְבֶּךָ, וְלֹא מִן הַסְּפָר. וְטַעֲמָא דִקְרָא, שֶׁמָּא יִשְׁמְעוּ נָכְרִים וְיָבֹאוּ וְיַחֲרִיבוּ אֶרֶץ יִשְׂרָאֵל, לְפִיכָךְ אֵין מַנִּיחִין הָעִיר תֵּל עוֹלָם כְּמִשְׁפַּט עִיר הַנִּדַּחַת, אֲבָל הוֹרְגִין אֶת יוֹשְׁבֶיהָ בִּלְבָד:


בספר – [on the border] a city that separates between the Land of Israel and the heathen countries, as it is written, “among you” (Deuteronomy 17:2), but not from the border. And the reason of the verse is, lest the heathens hear and come and lay waste/destroy the Land of Israel; therefore, we do not leave eternal mound of ruins according to the statute of the apostate city, but we only kill all of its inhabitants.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 1:5:11

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:5:11](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:5:11)


**וְלֹא שָׁלֹשׁ.** עֲיָרוֹת. עִיר הַנִּדַּחַת בְּבֵית דִּין אֶחָד וּבְמָקוֹם אֶחָד, כְּלוֹמַר קְרוֹבִים זוֹ לָזוֹ. אֲבָל בִּשְׁנַיִם וּשְׁלֹשָׁה מְקוֹמוֹת עוֹשִׂים:


ולא שלשה – [but not three] towns, the apostate city with one Jewish court and in one place. That is to say, each is near to the other, but with two or three places we do it.



### Mishnah 6

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 1:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:6:1)


**וּמֹשֶׁה עַל גַּבֵּיהֶם הֲרֵי שִׁבְעִים וְאֶחָד.** דְּאָמַר קְרָא (במדבר יא) וְנָשְׂאוּ אִתְּךָ, וְאַתְּ בַּהֲדַיְהוּ:


ומשה על גביהן הרי שבעים ואחד – for the Biblical verse states (Numbers 11:17): “they shall bear [the burden of the people] with you, [and you shall not bear it alone],” and they will be with you.”


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 1:6:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:6:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:6:2)


**רַבִּי יְהוּדָה אוֹמֵר שִׁבְעִים.** דְּדָרֵישׁ אִתְּךָ, בְּדוֹמִין לְךָ, וְלֹא שֶׁיִּהְיֶה הוּא יוֹשֵׁב עִמָּהֶן בַּדִּין. וְאֵין הֲלָכָה כְּרַבִּי יְהוּדָה:


רבי יהודה אומר: שבעים – Who expounds on the word אתך/with you, that are similar to you, and not that you will sit with them in judgement. But the Halakha does not follow Rabbi Yehuda.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 1:6:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:6:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:6:3)


**עֵדָה שׁוֹפֶטֶת.** עֲשָׂרָה מְחַיְּבִין:


עדה שופטת – ten who make liable/guilty


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 1:6:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:6:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:6:4)


**עֵדָה מַצֶּלֶת.** עֲשָׂרָה מְזַכִּין. וְשָׁמְעִינַן מִנַּהּ דְּצָרִיךְ שֶׁיִּהְיוּ עֶשְׂרִים, שֶׁאִם יֵחָלְקוּ יִהְיוּ עֲשָׂרָה מְחַיְּבִין וַעֲשָׂרָה מְזַכִּין:


עדה מצלת – ten who acquit, and learn from this that there needs to be twenty for if they divided it, there would be ten making liable and ten acquitting.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 1:6:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:6:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:6:5)


**הַטָּיָתְךָ לְרָעָה עַל פִּי שְׁנַיִם.** וְהָכִי קָאָמַר קְרָא, לֹא תִהְיֶה אַחֲרֵי רַבִּים לְרָעוֹת, לְחַיֵּב עַל פִּי אֶחָד שֶׁיִּהְיֶה יָתֵר עַל הַמְזַכִּין. אֲבָל אַחֲרֵי רַבִּים, לְהַטּוֹת בִּשְׁנַיִם, אֲפִלּוּ לְרָעוֹת, כְּשֶׁיִּהְיוּ שְׁנַיִם מְחַיְּבִין יוֹתֵר עַל הַמְזַכִּין. הִלְכָּךְ עַל כָּרְחֲךָ עֶשְׂרִים וּשְׁלֹשָׁה בָּעִינַן, דִּבְצִיר מֵעֲשָׂרָה מְזַכִּין לֵיכָּא לְמֵימַר, דְּהָא כְתִיב וְהִצִּילוּ הָעֵדָה, וְתוּ לֹא מַשְׁכַּחַת חוֹבָה בִּבְצִיר מִשְּׁנֵים עָשָׂר:


הטייתך לרעה על פי שנים – This is how the verse (Exodus 23:2) should be read: “You shall neither side with the mighty/multitude to do wrong” – to make guilty via a [majority of] one , so that there would be more than those who acquit; “but to pervert it in favor of the mighty” – with two, even for evil, so that there would be two more declaring guilty than those who acquit. Therefore, perforce, we require twenty-three [judges], for less than ten [judges] who acquit =, we don’t have to say, for it it is written (Numbers 35:25): “And the assembly shall protect (the manslayer from the blood avenger),” and further, we do not find that “guilt” is less than twelve.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 1:6:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:6:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:6:6)


**וְאֵין בֵּית דִּין שָׁקוּל.** אֵין עוֹשִׂין בֵּית דִּין זוּגוֹת, שֶׁאִם יֵחָלְקוּ לַחֲצָאִים הָוֵי לְהוּ פַּלְגָּא וּפַלְגָּא וְלֹא מַשְׁכַּחַת לַהּ הַטָּיָתְךָ לְטוֹבָה עַל פִּי אֶחָד. הִלְכָּךְ מוֹסִיפִין עֲלֵיהֶם עוֹד אֶחָד וְהָווּ לְהוּ עֶשְׂרִים וּשְׁלֹשָׁה:


ואין בית דין שקול – We don’t make pairs in Jewish courts, for it they would split tdown the middle, it would be half and half – and we would not find giving a verdict according to the majority of votes by a majority of one for good; hence, we add to them one other [judge] so that there would be twenty-three.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 1:6:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:6:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:6:7)


**מֵאָה וְעֶשְׂרִים.** מְפָרֵשׁ בַּגְּמָרָא, עֶשְׂרִים וּשְׁלֹשָׁה, סַנְהֶדְרִי קְטַנָּה. וְשָׁלֹשׁ שׁוּרוֹת שֶׁל עֶשְׂרִים וּשְׁלֹשָׁה עֶשְׂרִים וּשְׁלֹשָׁה יוֹשְׁבִים לִפְנֵיהֶם, שֶׁאִם הֻצְרְכוּ לְהוֹסִיף עַל הַדַּיָּנִים מוֹסִיפִים מֵהֶם. וַעֲשָׂרָה בַטְלָנִים, עֲשָׂרָה בְנֵי אָדָם בְּטֵלִים מִכָּל מְלָאכָה שֶׁיּוֹשְׁבִים תָּמִיד בְּבֵית הַמִּדְרָשׁ. וּשְׁנֵי סוֹפְרִים לִכְתֹּב דִּבְרֵי הַמְזַכִּין וְדִבְרֵי הַמְחַיְּבִין. וּשְׁנֵי חַזָּנִים, שַׁמָּשֵׁי בֵית דִּין לְהַלְקוֹת הַחַיָּב וּלְהַזְמִין בַּעֲלֵי הַדִּין. וּשְׁנֵי בַעֲלֵי דִינִין. וּשְׁנֵי עֵדִים. וּשְׁנֵי זוֹמְמִין. וּשְׁנֵי זוֹמְמֵי זוֹמְמִין. וּשְׁנֵי גַבָּאִים, וּשְׁלִישִׁי לְחַלֵּק הַצְּדָקָה, שֶׁצְּדָקָה נִגְבֵּית בִּשְׁנַיִם וּמִתְחַלֶּקֶת בִּשְׁלֹשָׁה. וְרוֹפֵא אֻמָּן לְהַקִּיז דָּם. וְלַבְלָר. וּמְלַמֵּד תִּינוֹקוֹת. הֲרֵי מֵאָה וְעֶשְׂרִים:


מאה ועשרים – The Gemara explains that twenty-three judges is a Small Sanhedrin. And three rows of twenty-three apiece sit before them, for if there was a need increase [the number of] judges, they would add from them, and there would be ten idle individuals [available]. These ten individuals are idle from all work and always sit in the House of Study. And two scribes [are available] who write the words of those who acquit and those who find guilty. And two men who announce the order of proceedings, sextons of the Jewish court who flog the guilty and invite the opponents in court, the two opponents [themselves], and two witnesses and two conspiring witnesses and two witnesses who find the previous witnesses to be conspiring, and two treasurers/managers [of the charity], and a third to distribute the tzedakah/charity, since charity is collected by two and distributed by a third, and an artisan doctor [whose task it is] to draw blood, and a scribe, and a teacher of children which [all together] equals one-hundred and twenty.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 1:6:8

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:6:8](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/1:6:8)


**מָאתַיִם וּשְׁלֹשִׁים כְּנֶגֶד שָׂרֵי עֲשָׂרוֹת.** דְּהַיְנוּ עֶשְׂרִים וּשְׁלֹשָׁה עֲשִׂירִיּוֹת, שֶׁיְּהֵא כָל דַּיָּן שַׂר שֶׁל עֲשָׂרָה. דִּבְצִיר מִשָּׂרֵי עֲשָׂרָה לֹא אַשְׁכְּחַן שְׂרָרָה. וְאֵין הֲלָכָה כְּרַבִּי נְחֶמְיָה:


מאתים ושלשים כדי שרי עשרות – since that is twenty three groups of ten, whereby each judge would be the prince of ten, for less than the princes of ten, we would not find rulership. But the Halakha does not follow Rabbi Nehemiah.



## Chapter 2


### Mishnah 1

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 2:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:1:1)


**כֹּהֵן גָּדוֹל.** אֵינוֹ יוֹצֵא אַחַר הַמִּטָּה. דִּלְמָא אָתֵי לְמִנְגַּע מִתּוֹךְ טִרְדָּתוֹ, וְרַחֲמָנָא אָמַר (ויקרא כא) וְעַל כָּל נַפְשֹׁת מֵת לֹא יָבֹא:


כהן גדול. אינו יוצא אחר המטה – Perhaps he would come to touch [the corpse] in the midst of his troubles. And the All-Merciful One said (Leviticus 21:11): “He shall not go where there is any dead body.”


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 2:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:1:2)


**הֵן נִכְסִין וְהוּא נִגְלֶה.** כֵּיוָן שֶׁנִּתְכַּסּוּ נוֹשְׂאֵי הַמִּטָּה מִן הַמָּבוֹי, כְּלוֹמַר שֶׁיָּצְאוּ מִמֶּנּוּ, הוּא נִגְלֶה וְנִכְנָס בְּתוֹכוֹ. אֲבָל כָּל זְמַן שֶׁהֵן נִגְלִין וְנִרְאִין בַּמָּבוֹי, הוּא נִכְסֶה מִמֶּנּוּ, שֶׁאֵינוֹ נִכְנָס לְתוֹכוֹ:


הן נכסין והוא נגלה – since those who carry the bier are covered from the entrance to a group of buildings/alleyway that is to say, when they left him; he was revealed and went inside within, but all the while that they were revealed and appeared in the alleyway, he was covered from them and did not enter into their midst.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 2:1:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:1:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:1:3)


**וְיוֹצֵא עִמָּהֶן עַד פֶּתַח הָעִיר.** שֶׁבָּעִיר מְצוּיִין מְבוֹאוֹת וְיָכוֹל לְהִתְכַּסּוֹת מֵהֶן. אֲבָל חוּץ לָעִיר לֵיכָּא הֶכֵּרָא:


ויוצא עמהן עד פתח העיר – that in the city were found alleyways and he can cover himself from them, but outside of the city, there is no recognition.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 2:1:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:1:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:1:4)


**שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר וּמִן הַמִּקְדָּשׁ לֹא יֵצֵא.** רַבִּי יְהוּדָה דָרֵישׁ וּמִן הַמִּקְדָּשׁ לֹא יֵצֵא כָל עִקָּר. וְרַבִּי מֵאִיר דָּרֵישׁ וּמִקְּדֻשָּׁתוֹ לֹא יֵצֵא, כְּלוֹמַר יִזְדְּהַר שֶׁלֹּא יָבֹא לִידֵי מַגָּע, וּבְתוֹךְ הָעִיר שֶׁיֵּשׁ מְבוֹאוֹת אִיכָּא הֶכֵּרָא וּמִזְדְּהַר. וַהֲלָכָה כְּרַבִּי יְהוּדָה:


שנאמר: "ומן המקדש לא יצא" – Rabbi Yehuda expounds [on the verse – Leviticus 21:12]: “He shall not go outside the sanctuary,” at all. And Rabbi Meir expounds that he shall not leave from his holiness, that is, he should be careful that he not come in contact [with the bier] and within the city there are alleys where there is recognition and he is careful. And the Halakha is according to Rabbi Yehuda.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 2:1:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:1:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:1:5)


**וּכְשֶׁהוּא מְנַחֵם אֶת אֲחֵרִים.** דִּבְמֵת שֶׁאֵינוֹ שֶׁלּוֹ כֻּלֵּי עָלְמָא מוֹדוּ דְּיָכוֹל הוּא לֵילֵךְ, דְּלֹא טְרִיד וְלֹא אָתֵי לְמִנְגַּע. וּכְשֶׁחוֹזְרִין מִן הַקְּבָרוֹת וְעוֹמְדִין בְּשׁוּרָה לְנַחֵם הָאֲבֵלִים וְכָל הָעָם עוֹבְרִים זֶה אַחַר זֶה וּמְנַחֲמִין הָאָבֵל שֶׁעוֹמֵד בְּמַעֲמָדוֹ וְכָל אֶחָד אוֹמֵר לוֹ תִּתְנַחֵם מִן הַשָּׁמַיִם:


וכשהוא מנם את אחרים – that is of a dead person that is not his (family), everyone admits that he is able to go and not go to the trouble that he won’t come in contact [with the bier]. And when they return from the graves and stand in a line to comfort the mourners and all the people pass one after another and comfort the mourner who stands at his standing post [to receive the consolation of the others in attendance] , and everyone says to him: “May you be comforted from Heaven.”


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 2:1:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:1:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:1:6)


**וְהַמְמֻנֶּה מְמַצְּעוֹ.** שֶׁהוּא הוֹלֵךְ לִימִין כֹּהֵן גָּדוֹל וְכָל הָעָם מִשְּׂמֹאלוֹ, נִמְצָא כֹּהֵן גָּדוֹל בָּאֶמְצַע:


הממונה – he is the deputy [High Priest] , who is appointed to serve in place of the High Priest, lest something occur to him that would make the High Priest unfit for service on Yom Kippur.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 2:1:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:1:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:1:7)


**הַמְמֻנֶּה.** הוּא הַסְּגָן, שֶׁהוּא מְמֻנֶּה לַעֲבֹד תַּחַת כֹּהֵן גָּדוֹל אִם יֶאֱרַע פְּסוּל בְּכֹהֵן גָּדוֹל בְּיוֹם הַכִּפּוּרִים:


והממונה מנצעו – who walks to the right of the High Priest and all the people are to his left, so that the High Priest is in the middle.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 2:1:8

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:1:8](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:1:8)


**אָנוּ כַפָּרָתְךָ.** בָּנוּ תִתְכַּפֵּר אַתָּה וַאֲנַחְנוּ תַחְתֶּיךָ לְכָל הָרָאוּי לָבֹא עָלֶיךָ:


אנו כפרתך – though us you will be forgiven – you and we under you for all that is appropriate to come upon you.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 2:1:9

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:1:9](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:1:9)


**וּכְשֶׁמַּבְרִין אוֹתוֹ.** שֶׁאָבֵל אָסוּר לֶאֱכֹל סְעֻדָּה רִאשׁוֹנָה מִשֶּׁלּוֹ, וּקְרוֹבָיו וְאוֹהֲבָיו מַאֲכִילִין אוֹתוֹ:


וכשמברין אותו – for a mourner is forbidden to eat the first meal of his own [possessions], but his relatives and loved ones/friends feed him.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 2:1:10

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:1:10](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:1:10)


**מְסֻבִּין עַל הָאָרֶץ.** הֵן מְצֵרִין וּמִתְאַבְּלִין בְּצַעֲרוֹ:


מסובין על הארץ – they distress themselves and mourn in his grief/pain.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 2:1:11

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:1:11](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:1:11)


**וְהוּא מֵסֵב.** בְּכָבוֹד עַל הַסַּפְסָל:


והוא מיסב – with honor on his bench.



### Mishnah 2

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 2:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:2:1)


**הַמֶּלֶךְ לֹא דָן וְלֹא דָנִין אוֹתוֹ.** וְדַוְקָא מַלְכֵי יִשְׂרָאֵל שֶׁאֵינָם נִשְׁמָעִים לְדִבְרֵי חֲכָמִים. אֲבָל מֶלֶךְ מִמַּלְכֵי בֵית דָּוִד, דָּן וְדָנִין אוֹתוֹ, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר (ירמיה כא) בֵּית דָּוִד כֹּה אָמַר ה'‏ דִּינוּ לַבֹּקֶר מִשְׁפָּט:


המלך לא דן ולא דנין אותו – and specifically the kings of Israel who do not listen to the words of the Sages. But a king from the kings of the House of David, [serve as] judges and we judge him, as it says (Jeremiah 21:12): “O House of David, thus said the LORD: Render just verdicts morning by morning…”


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 2:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:2:2)


**אֵין שׁוֹמְעִין לוֹ.** דְּמֶלֶךְ שֶׁמָּחַל עַל כְּבוֹדוֹ אֵין כְּבוֹדוֹ מָחוּל, וּגְנַאי הוּא לוֹ שֶׁתַּחְלֹץ וְיָרְקָה בְּפָנָיו. וְכָל שֶׁאֵינָהּ בַּת חֲלִיצָה אֵינָהּ בַּת יִבּוּם. וְכֵן הֲלָכָה:


אין שומעין לו – since a king who has forgone the honor due to oneself, his honor is not remitted and it is a disgrace to him that he should loosen his shoe and she should spit in his face. And whomever is not someone who should undergo the ceremony of removing the shoe, she also is not someone who should undergo levirate marriage, and such is the Halakha.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 2:2:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:2:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:2:3)


**מֶלֶךְ נוֹשֵׂא אַלְמָנָתוֹ שֶׁל מֶלֶךְ.** וַהֲלָכָה כְּרַבִּי יְהוּדָה בְּהָא:


מלך נושא אלמנתו של מלך – And the halakha is according to Rabbi Yehuda in this.



### Mishnah 3

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 2:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:3:1)


**אֵינוֹ יוֹצֵא מִפֶּתַח פַּלְטְרִין שֶׁלּוֹ.** שֶׁגְּנַאי הוּא לַמֶּלֶךְ לְהַרְאוֹת עָגְמַת נֶפֶשׁ לִפְנֵי הָעָם:


אינו יוצא מפתח פלטרין שלו – for it is a disgrace for the king to show his grief before the people.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 2:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:3:2)


**לְפַיֵּס אֶת הָעָם.** כְּדֵי שֶׁיַּכִּירוּ שֶׁלֹּא בַעֲצַת דָּוִד הָרַג יוֹאָב אֶת אַבְנֵר. וְאֵין הֲלָכָה כְּרַבִּי יְהוּדָה:


לפייס את העם – in order that they should know that it was not at the David’s advice that Joab should kill Avner [son of Ner, the military commander of Saul] (See 2 Samuel 3:37). And the Halakha is not according to Rabbi Yehuda.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 2:3:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:3:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:3:3)


**דַּרְגָּשׁ.** מִטָּה:


דרגש – bed



### Mishnah 4

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 2:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:4:1)


**לְמִלְחֶמֶת הָרְשׁוּת.** מִלְחֶמֶת שְׁאָר אֻמּוֹת חוּץ מִמִּלְחֶמֶת עֲמָלֵק וּמִלְחֶמֶת שִׁבְעָה עֲמָמִין:


למלחמת הרשות – The wars of the rest of the nations, except for the war vs. Amalek and the War of the seven [Canaanite] nations.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 2:4:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:4:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:4:2)


**וּפוֹרֵץ.** גָּדֵר שֶׁל אֲחֵרִים:


ופורץ – the fence of others


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 2:4:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:4:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:4:3)


**לַעֲשׂוֹת לוֹ דֶרֶךְ.** לָלֶכֶת לְכַרְמוֹ וּלְשָׂדֵהוּ:


לעשות לו דרך – to go to his vineyard and to his field


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 2:4:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:4:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:4:4)


**וְאֵין מְמַחִין בְּיָדוֹ.** אֵין מְעַכְּבִין עָלָיו:


אין ממחין בידו – we don’t prevent him


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 2:4:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:4:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:4:5)


**חֵלֶק בָּרֹאשׁ.** חֵלֶק הַיָּפֶה בּוֹרֵר רִאשׁוֹן. וְנוֹטֵל מֶחֱצָה מִכָּל הַבִּזָּה:


חלק בראש – he chooses the best portion first and takes one half of all the spoil.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 2:4:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:4:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:4:6)


**אֶלָּא שְׁמֹנֶה עֶשְׂרֵה.** שֶׁהֲרֵי דָוִד הָיוּ לוֹ שֵׁשׁ נָשִׁים וְקָאָמַר לֵיהּ נָבִיא וְאִם מְעָט וְאֹסִיפָה לְּךָ כָּהֵנָּה וְכָהֵנָּה, כָּהֵנָּה קַמָּא שִׁית, בַּתְרָא שִׁית, הֲרֵי שְׁמֹנֶה עֶשְׂרֵה:


אלא שמונה עשרה – for David had had six wives as the prophet [Nathan] said to him (2 Samuel 12:8): “[I gave you your master’s house and possession of your master’s wives; and I gave you the House of Israel and Judah] and if that were not enough, I would give you twice as much more.” The first mention of כהנה – is six and the latter [mention of] כהנה – is [another] six, that makes eighteen [wives].


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 2:4:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:4:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:4:7)


**מַרְבֶּה הוּא לוֹ.** יוֹתֵר מִשְּׁמֹנֶה עֶשְׂרֵה. וְשָׁלֹשׁ מַחֲלוֹקוֹת יֵשׁ בַּדָּבָר. תַּנָּא קַמָּא סָבַר נוֹשֵׂא שְׁמֹנֶה עֶשְׂרֵה וַאֲפִלּוּ פְרוּצוֹת, וְיוֹתֵר מִשְּׁמֹנֶה עֶשְׂרֵה לֹא יִשָּׂא וַאֲפִלּוּ כְשֵׁרוֹת, שֶׁכָּךְ הוּא גְזֵרַת הַכָּתוּב. רַבִּי יְהוּדָה סָבַר נוֹשֵׂא שְׁמֹנֶה עֶשְׂרֵה וַאֲפִלּוּ פְרוּצוֹת, וְיוֹתֵר מִשְּׁמֹנֶה עֶשְׂרֵה פְרוּצוֹת לֹא יִשָּׂא, וּכְשֵׁרוֹת וַהֲגוּנוֹת יִשָּׂא כְמוֹ שֶׁיִּרְצֶה. וּפָלֵיג אַתַּנָּא קַמָּא בַּחֲדָא. רַבִּי שִׁמְעוֹן סָבַר אֲפִלּוּ אַחַת פְּרוּצָה לֹא יִשָּׂא, וּכְשֵׁרוֹת וַהֲגוּנוֹת לֹא יִשָּׂא יוֹתֵר מִשְּׁמֹנֶה עֶשְׂרֵה. וּפָלֵיג אַתַּנָּא קַמָּא בַּחֲדָא וְאַדְּרַבִּי יְהוּדָה בְּתַרְתֵּי. וַהֲלָכָה כְּתַנָּא קַמָּא:


מרבה הוא לו – More than eighteen. And there three disputations in this matter. The first Tanna/teacher holds that he marries eighteen, and even if they are loose in moral conduct, but more than eighteen he should not marry even if they are valid, for that is the decree of the Biblical verse. Rabbi Yehuda holds that he marries eighteen and even if they are loose in moral conduct, but more than eighteen who are loose in moral conduct, he should not marry, but if they are valid and worthy, he may marry as many as he wishes. And he disagrees with the first Tanna in one matter. Rabbi Shimon states that even one [wife] who is loose in moral conduct, he should not marry, but valid and worthy [wives], he cannot marry more than eighteen. And he disagrees with the first Tanna in one matter and with Rabbi Yehuda in two matters. And the Halakha is according to the first Tanna.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 2:4:8

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:4:8](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:4:8)


**אֶלָּא כְדֵי מֶרְכַּבְתּוֹ.** דַּוְקָא סוּסִים בְּטֵלִים לְהַרְחִיב דַּעְתּוֹ וּלְהִתְגַּדֵּל בְּרֹב סוּסִים [אָסוּר]. אֲבָל כְּדֵי רִכְבּוֹ וּפָרָשָׁיו לְהִלָּחֵם בְּאוֹיְבָיו מֻתָּר:


אלא כדי מרכבתו – and specifically idle horses to give him comfort and to increase the mass of horses, is prohibited. But, in order that his chariots and horsemen can fight his enemies, it is permitted.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 2:4:9

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:4:9](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:4:9)


**אַפְסַנְיָא.** שְׂכַר חֵילוֹת הַנִּכְנָסִים וְיוֹצְאִים עִמּוֹ כָל הַשָּׁנָה:


אספניא – the salary of the soldiers who come in and go out with him throughout the year.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 2:4:10

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:4:10](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:4:10)


**וְכוֹתֵב לוֹ סֵפֶר תּוֹרָה לִשְׁמוֹ.** חוּץ מִסֵּפֶר תּוֹרָה שֶׁחַיָּב כָּל אָדָם מִיִּשְׂרָאֵל לִהְיוֹת לוֹ סֵפֶר תּוֹרָה, וְהוּא מֻנָּח בְּבֵית גְּנָזָיו. וְסֵפֶר תּוֹרָה שֶׁכּוֹתֵב לִשְׁמוֹ כְשֶׁהוּא מֶלֶךְ נִכְנָס וְיוֹצֵא עִמּוֹ תָמִיד:


וכותב לו ס"ת לשמו – besides the Torah scroll that every Jewish person is required to have with him, and it is placed in his storehouse. And the Torah scroll that he writes for himself when he is King always enters and goes out with him.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 2:4:11

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:4:11](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:4:11)


**מֵסֵב.** עַל הַשֻּׁלְחָן:


מיסב – on the table



### Mishnah 5

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 2:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/2:5:1)


**כְּשֶׁהוּא מִסְתַּפֵּר.** כְּשֶׁמְּגַלֵּחַ שְׂעַר רֹאשׁוֹ:


כשהוא מסתפר – when he shaves the hair of his head



## Chapter 3


### Mishnah 1

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 3:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/3:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/3:1:1)


**זֶה בּוֹרֵר לוֹ אֶחָד.** אֶחָד מֵהַבַּעֲלֵי דִינִין בּוֹרֵר לוֹ דַּיָּן אֶחָד לְדוּנוֹ וּלְהַפֵּךְ בִּזְכוּתוֹ, וְכֵן הַשֵּׁנִי בּוֹרֵר לוֹ דַיָּן אֶחָד. וּשְׁנֵי הַבַּעֲלֵי דִינִים בְּיַחַד בּוֹרְרִים לָהֶם עוֹד דַּיָּן שְׁלִישִׁי, וּמִתּוֹךְ כָּךְ יֵצֵא דִין אֱמֶת לַאֲמִתּוֹ, דְּצָיְתֵי בַעֲלֵי דִינִים דִּינָא וְאָמְרֵי קוּשְׁטָא קָא דָיְנֵי לָן, דְּסָבַר הַחַיָּב הֲרֵי אֲנִי בְעַצְמִי בָּרַרְתִּי הָאֶחָד וְאִם הָיָה יָכוֹל לְהַפֵּךְ בִּזְכוּתִי הָיָה מְהַפֵּךְ. וְהַדַּיָּן הַשְּׁלִישִׁי בְּעַצְמוֹ נוֹחַ לוֹ לְהַפֵּךְ בִּזְכוּת שְׁנֵיהֶם, מִפְּנֵי שֶׁשְּׁנֵיהֶם בָּרְרוּ אוֹתוֹ:


זה בורר לו אחד – One of the litigants selects for himself one judge for adjudicate for him and change his verdict to innocence. And similarly, the second [litigant] chooses for himself a judge, and two of the litigants together choose another third judge, and through this, a faithful and truthful judgment will result, for both litigants will listen the judgment and they will say that truth was adjudicated for us. For the guilty party will hold: “Behold, on my own, I chose the one, and if he is able to change the verdict to innocence, he would change it. And the third judge on his own is willing to change the verdict to innocence for both of them , because both of them (i.e., the litigants) chose him.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 3:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/3:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/3:1:2)


**וַחֲכָמִים אוֹמְרִים שְׁנֵי הַדַּיָּנִים בּוֹרְרִים לָהֶם אֶחָד.** בְּלֹא דַעַת הַבַּעֲלֵי דִינִים. כְּדֵי שֶׁלֹּא יִהְיֶה לִבּוֹ שֶׁל זֶה הַשְּׁלִישִׁי נוֹטֶה אֵצֶל אֶחָד מֵהֶן. וַהֲלָכָה כַּחֲכָמִים:


וחכמים אומרים: שני הדיינים בוררים להם אחד – without the knowledge of the litigants, in order that the heart of the third judge would not lean towards one of them. And the Halakha is according to the Sages.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 3:1:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/3:1:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/3:1:3)


**זֶה פּוֹסֵל דַּיָּנוֹ שֶׁל זֶה.** יָכוֹל הוּא לוֹמַר לֹא אָדוּן לִפְנֵי בֵית דִּין שֶׁבָּרַרְתָּ:


זה פוסל דיינו של זה – He can say, I will not judge before the Jewish court that you have selected


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 3:1:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/3:1:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/3:1:4)


**בִּזְמַן שֶׁמֵּבִיא עֲלֵיהֶן רְאָיָה.** שֶׁזֶּה מֵבִיא רְאָיָה עַל דַּיָּן שֶׁבָּרַר זֶה, לְפָסְלוֹ:


בזמן שהביא עליהן ראיה – for this one brings evidence on one judge that he selected to invalidate him.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 3:1:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/3:1:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/3:1:5)


**אֲבָל אִם הֵם כְּשֵׁרִים וּמֻמְחִים.** הָכִי קָאָמַר, אֲבָל אִם הָיוּ כְשֵׁרִים שֶׁאֵינָן לֹא קְרוֹבִים וְלֹא פְסוּלִים, אַף עַל פִּי שֶׁהֵם יוֹשְׁבֵי קְרָנוֹת נַעֲשׂוּ כְמֻמְחִין וְאֵינוֹ יָכוֹל לְפָסְלָן. וּפְסַק הַהֲלָכָה בָזֶה, כְּשֶׁקִּבְּלוּ בַעֲלֵי הַדִּין מִי שֶׁיָּדִין לָהֶם בֵּין יָחִיד בֵּין רַבִּים, וּפָסַק עֲלֵיהֶם אֶת הַדִּין, דִּינוֹ דִין וְאֵין סוֹתְרִין דִּינוֹ וְאַף עַל פִּי שֶׁאֵינוֹ מֻמְחֶה לָרַבִּים. וְאִם נוֹדַע שֶׁטָּעָה, אִם בִּדְבַר מִשְׁנָה טָעָה אוֹ בְדָבָר הַמְפֹרָשׁ בַּגְּמָרָא, מַחֲזִירִים הַדָּבָר כְּשֶׁהָיָה וְדָנִין בּוֹ כַהֲלָכָה. וְאִם אִי אֶפְשָׁר לְהַחֲזִיר, כְּגוֹן שֶׁהָלַךְ זֶה שֶׁנָּטַל הַמָּמוֹן שֶׁלֹּא כַדִּין לִמְדִינַת הַיָּם, פָּטוּר הַדַּיָּן מִלְּשַׁלֵּם מֵאַחַר שֶׁקִּבְּלוּהוּ עֲלֵיהֶם, שֶׁאַף עַל פִּי שֶׁגָּרַם לְהַזִּיק, לֹא נִתְכַּוֵּן לְהַזִּיק. וְאִם טָעָה בְשִׁקּוּל הַדַּעַת, וְהוּא בְדָבָר שֶׁנֶּחְלְקוּ בוֹ תַנָּאִים אוֹ אָמוֹרָאִים אוֹ גְאוֹנִים, וְסוּגְיָא דְעָלְמָא כְחַד מִנַּיְהוּ וְדָן זֶה הַדַּיָּן כְּדִבְרֵי אוֹתוֹ הַגָּאוֹן שֶׁאֵין סוּגְיַת הָעוֹלָם כְּמוֹתוֹ, אִם לֹא נָשָׂא וְנָתַן בַּיָּד יַחֲזֹר הַדִּין, וְאִם אִי אֶפְשָׁר לְהַחֲזִיר יְשַׁלֵּם מִבֵּיתוֹ, וְאִם נָשָׂא וְנָתַן בַּיָּד, מַה שֶּׁעָשָׂה עָשׂוּי וִישַׁלֵּם מִבֵּיתוֹ. וְדַיָּן שֶׁלֹּא קִבְּלוּ אוֹתוֹ בַעֲלֵי הַדִּין אֶלָּא שֶׁעָמַד מֵאֵלָיו אוֹ מִנָּה אוֹתוֹ הַמֶּלֶךְ אוֹ קְצָת זִקְנֵי הַקָּהָל הֶעֱמִידוּהוּ, אִם אֵינוֹ מֻמְחֶה לָרַבִּים, אַף עַל פִּי שֶׁנָּטַל רְשׁוּת מֵרֹאשׁ הַגּוֹלָה, אֵין דִּינָיו דִּין, בֵּין טָעָה בֵּין לֹא טָעָה, וְאֵינוֹ בִכְלַל הַדַּיָּנִין אֶלָּא בִכְלַל בַּעֲלֵי זְרוֹעַ, וְכָל אֶחָד מִבַּעֲלֵי דִינִין אִם רָצָה סוֹתֵר דִּינוֹ וְחוֹזֵר וְדָן בִּפְנֵי בֵית דִּין. וְאִם טָעָה וְלֹא נָשָׂא וְנָתַן בַּיָּד יַחֲזִיר הַדִּין, וְאִם אִי אֶפְשָׁר לְהַחֲזִיר יְשַׁלֵּם מִבֵּיתוֹ כְּדִין כָּל גּוֹרֵם לְהַזִּיק, וְאִם נָשָׂא וְנָתַן בַּיָּד יְשַׁלֵּם מִבֵּיתוֹ וְחוֹזֵר וְנוֹטֵל מִבַּעַל דִּין זֶה שֶׁנָּתַן לוֹ שֶׁלֹּא כַהֲלָכָה. וּמֻמְחֶה לָרַבִּים שֶׁקִּבְּלוּ אוֹתוֹ בַעֲלֵי דִינִים אוֹ שֶׁנָּטַל רְשׁוּת מֵרֹאשׁ גּוֹלָה אַף עַל פִּי שֶׁלֹּא קִבְּלוּ אוֹתוֹ בַעֲלֵי דִינִים, הוֹאִיל וְהוּא מֻמְחֶה, אִם טָעָה בֵּין בִּדְבַר מִשְׁנָה בֵּין בְּשִׁקּוּל הַדַּעַת וְאִי אֶפְשָׁר לְהַחֲזִיר הַדִּין, פָּטוּר מִלְּשַׁלֵּם. וּמֻמְחֶה שֶׁנָּטַל רְשׁוּת מֵרֹאשׁ גּוֹלָה יֵשׁ לוֹ לָכוֹף לְבַעֲלֵי הַדִּין שֶׁיָּדוּנוּ לְפָנָיו, בֵּין רָצוּ בֵּין לֹא רָצוּ, בֵּין בָּאָרֶץ בֵּין בְּחוּץ לָאָרֶץ. וּמִי שֶׁנָּטַל רְשׁוּת מִן הַנָּשִׂיא שֶׁבְּאֶרֶץ יִשְׂרָאֵל אֵינוֹ יָכוֹל לָכוֹף לְבַעֲלֵי הַדִּין אֶלָּא בְאֶרֶץ יִשְׂרָאֵל בִּלְבָד. וּמֻמְחֶה הוּא מִי שֶׁלָּמַד בַּתּוֹרָה שֶׁבִּכְתָב וְשֶׁבְּעַל פֶּה וְיוֹדֵעַ לִסְבֹּר לְהַקִּישׁ וּלְהָבִין דָּבָר מִתּוֹךְ דָּבָר [הוּא הַנִּקְרָא מֻמְחֶה]. וּכְשֶׁהוּא נִכָּר וְיָדוּעַ וְיָצָא טִבְעוֹ אֶל אַנְשֵׁי דוֹרוֹ, נִקְרָא מֻמְחֶה לָרַבִּים, וְהוּא יָכוֹל לָדוּן יְחִידִי וַאֲפִלּוּ לֹא נָקַט רְשׁוּת מֵרֹאשׁ הַגּוֹלָה:


אבל אם הם כשרים ומומחים – This is how it should be understood: But if they were worthy in that they are neither relatives nor invalid, even though they sit on street corners, they became like specialists and he cannot invalidate them. The Halakhic decision on this is that when the litigants accepted who would judge them, whether for an individual or for the public, and he made a legal decision for them, his judgment is law and they cannot reverse his judgment even though he is not a specialist recognized by the public, and if it is known that he erred, if it is something from the Mishnah that he erred in or something explicitly written in the Gemara, they revoke the matter and re-judge it according to Halakha. But if it is impossible to revoke the matter such as the case that the person who took the money illegally went abroad, the judge is exempt form payment after they had accepted him [as judge] over them, for even though he caused for damage [to be done], he did not intend to cause damage. And if he had erred in his weighing between opposing views, and this is a matter that the Tannaim or Amoraim or Gaonim disputed about, but the general practice is like one off them, and the judge adjudicated like according to the words of that Gaon whose opinion was not like the general practice, if he did not engage in give and take by hand, he should retract the judgment, but if it is impossible to retract, he should pay from his own estate, and if he engaged in give-and-take by hand , what is done is done and he should pay from his own estate. But a judge whom the litigants did not accept but stood up on his own [to be a judge] or a king appointed him or some of the elders of the community appointed him, if he is not a specialist recognized by the public, even though he received permission from the Exilarch his judgments are not law, whether he erred or whether he did not err. And he is not amongst the judges but rather amongst those who act by force, and each one of the litigants if he wants, he reverses the decision and the judgment returns before the Jewish court. And if he erred and did not engage in give-and-take by hand, the judgment should be reversed, and if it is impossible to reverse it, he should pay from his estate according to the law of all who cause damage, and if he engaged in give-and-take by hand he should pay from his estate and return from what the litigant gave him which was not according to Halakha. But specialist recognized by the public whom the litigants accepted or that he received permission [to judge] from the Exilarch even though the litigants did not accept him, for such he is a specialist, if he erred in a matter whether from the Mishnah or from making a decision after weighing between opposing views, and it is impossible to reverse the decision, he is exempt from making payment. And a specialist who received permission from the Exilarch must force the litigants before him that they will adjudicate before him, whether they wanted to or did not want to, whether in the land of Israel or outside the land of Israel. And whomever received permission from the Nasi in the land of Israel cannot force the litigants other than only in the land of Israel. And he who is a specialist, is one who learned the Written Torah and the Oral Torah and knows how to think, to make analogies and to understand a thing from the midst of another thing (and he is called a specialist). And when he is recognized and known and his character/substance has gone out [and become known] among the men of his generation, he is called an expert recognized by the public, and he may judge singularly (i.e., alone) and even if he did not receive permission from the Exilarch.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 3:1:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/3:1:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/3:1:6)


**זֶה פּוֹסֵל עֵדָיו שֶׁל זֶה.** פְּלוּגְתַיְהוּ דְרַבִּי מֵאִיר וְרַבָּנָן מוֹקֵי לַהּ בַּגְּמָרָא, כְּשֶׁאָמַר בַּעַל דִּין יֵשׁ לִי שְׁתֵּי כִתֵּי עֵדִים בַּדָּבָר, וְהֵבִיא כַת רִאשׁוֹנָה, וְעָמַד בַּעַל דִּין שֶׁכְּנֶגְדּוֹ עִם אַחֵר וְאָמַר פְּסוּלִין הֵן. רַבִּי מֵאִיר אוֹמֵר יָכוֹל הוּא עִם אַחֵר לְפָסְלָן, וְלָאו נוֹגֵעַ בָּעֵדוּת הוּא שֶׁהֲרֵי אָמַר יֵשׁ לִי כַת אַחֶרֶת. וְאִם בִּקֵּשׁ וְלֹא מָצָא, יַפְסִיד. וְרַבָּנָן סָבְרֵי אַף עַל גַּב דְּאָמַר תְּחִלָּה יֵשׁ לִי שְׁתֵּי כִתֵּי עֵדִים יָכוֹל לַחֲזֹר וְלוֹמַר אֵין לִי אֶלָּא אֵלּוּ, וְנִמְצָא זֶה שֶׁבָּא לְפָסְלָן נוֹגֵעַ בָּעֵדוּת הוּא, וְלֹא מִפַּסְלֵי אַפֻּמֵיהּ. וַהֲלָכָה כַּחֲכָמִים:


זה פוסל עדיו של זה – This is a dispute between Rabbi Meir and the Rabbis which is maintained in the Gemara, for when a litigant said: “I have two sets of witnesses in the manner” and he brings the first set and the opposing litigant stands with one another [person] and says, they are invalid, Rabbi Meir states he and another [person] may invalidate them, and he is not an interested witness, for he said that he has another set [of witnesses], but if he requested and did not find [them] he loses. And the Rabbis think that even though he said at first: “I have two sets of witnesses,” he can retract and say “I do not have ought of these, and the person who comes to invalidate them is an interested witness but they are not invalidated by his mouth. And the Halakha is according to the Sages.



### Mishnah 2

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 3:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/3:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/3:2:1)


**נֶאֱמָן עָלַי אַבָּא.** לִהְיוֹת דַּיָּן, אַף עַל פִּי שֶׁהוּא פָסוּל מִן הַתּוֹרָה לְדוּנֵנִי לֹא לִזְכוּת וְלֹא לְחוֹבָה, כִּדְנָפְקָא לָן מִלֹּא יוּמְתוּ אָבוֹת עַל בָּנִים:


נאמן עלי אבא – to be a judge. Even though he is ineligible from the Torah to judge me neither for acquittal nor for guilt, as we derive it from (Deuteronomy 24:16): “Parents shall not e put to death for children [nor children be put to death for parents: a person shall be put to death for his own crime].”


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 3:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/3:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/3:2:2)


**נֶאֱמָנִים עָלַי שְׁלֹשָׁה רוֹעֵי בָקָר.** לָדוּן. דְּאִלּוּ לְעֵדוּת, רוֹעֵי בָקָר כְּשֵׁרִים הֵן:


נאמנים עלי שלשה רועי בקר – to judge, for if it were for giving testimony, shepherds of cattle are valid.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 3:2:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/3:2:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/3:2:3)


**רַבִּי מֵאִיר אוֹמֵר יָכוֹל לַחֲזֹר בּוֹ.** אֲפִלּוּ לְאַחַר גְּמַר דִּין, לְאַחַר שֶׁקִּבֵּל הַדַּיָּן הָעֵדוּת וְאָמַר אִישׁ פְּלוֹנִי אַתָּה זַכַּאי:


רבי מאיר אומר: יכול לחזור בו – even after the final decision has been rendered, after the judge has received and the testimony and declared that so-and-so, you are innocent/acquitted.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 3:2:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/3:2:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/3:2:4)


**אֵינוֹ יָכוֹל לַחֲזֹר בּוֹ.** לְאַחַר גְּמַר דִּין בִּלְבָד הוּא דִפְלִיגֵי רַבָּנָן עֲלֵיהּ דְּרַבִּי מֵאִיר. דְּאִלּוּ קֹדֶם גְּמַר דִּין מוֹדִים חֲכָמִים לְרַבִּי מֵאִיר דְּיָכוֹל לַחֲזֹר בּוֹ. וְאִם קָנוּ מִיָּדוֹ נַמִּי שֶׁמְּקַבֵּל עָלָיו עֵדוּת אִישׁ פְּלוֹנִי אוֹ דִינוֹ שֶׁל אִישׁ פְּלוֹנִי, אֲפִלּוּ קֹדֶם גְּמַר דִּין אֵינוֹ יָכוֹל לַחֲזֹר בּוֹ, שֶׁאֵין לְאַחַר קִנְיָן כְּלוּם. וְכֵן הֲלָכָה:


אינו יכול לחזור בו – after the final decision has been rendered alone, that the Rabbis dispute the [opinion of] Rabbi Meir, for prior to the final decision being rendered,, the Sages agree with Rabbi Meir that he can retract. And also if they acquired [something] from his hand, when he receives the testimony of a certain person or the judgment of a certain person, even prior to rendering a final decision, he cannot retract, because there is nothing after an affirmation/acquisition (by handing over an object from one to the other contracting parties). And this is the Halakha.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 3:2:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/3:2:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/3:2:5)


**דּוֹר לִי בְחַיֵּי רֹאשְׁךָ.** נְדֹר לִי בְחַיֵּי רֹאשְׁךָ וְאֶתֵּן לְךָ מַה שֶּׁאַתָּה תוֹבֵעַ. וְאֵין צָרִיךְ לוֹמַר וּמָחוּל לְךָ מַה שֶּׁיֵּשׁ לִי אֶצְלְךָ. וְנָדַר לוֹ אוֹ קָנוּ מִיָּדוֹ אַף עַל פִּי שֶׁעֲדַיִן לֹא נָדַר, אֵינוֹ יָכוֹל לַחֲזֹר בּוֹ, כְּדִבְרֵי חֲכָמִים. וְכֵן הֲלָכָה:


דור לי בחיי ראשך – Let us make a vow by the life of your head and I will give you what you demand/claim, and you do not have to say: “You are remitted for what I have that is with you.” And he makes a vow or acquires it from his hand, even though he had not yet taken a vow, he may not retract, according to the words of the Sages. And the Halakha is according to the Sages.



### Mishnah 3

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 3:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/3:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/3:3:1)


**וְאֵלּוּ הֵן הַפְּסוּלִים.** לָדוּן וּלְהָעִיד:


ואלו הן הפסולים – to judge or to testify [as witnesses].


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 3:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/3:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/3:3:2)


**הַמְשַׂחֵק בְּקֻבְיָא.** פָּסוּל לְעֵדוּת לְפִי שֶׁאֵינוֹ מִתְעַסֵּק בְּיִשּׁוּבוֹ שֶׁל עוֹלָם, וְאָסוּר לְאָדָם שֶׁיִּתְעַסֵּק בְּעוֹלָמוֹ אֶלָּא אוֹ בְתוֹרָה וּגְמִילוּת חֲסָדִים, אוֹ בִסְחוֹרָה וְאֻמָּנוּת וּמְלָאכָה שֶׁיֵּשׁ בָּהֶן יִשּׁוּבוֹ שֶׁל עוֹלָם:


המשחק בקוביא – is ineligible for testimony because he does not engage in the welfare of the world. For it is forbidden for a person that he should engage himself other than in [the study of] Torah and deeds of lovingkindness, or in business and crafts or work which have [as its purpose] the welfare of the world.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 3:3:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/3:3:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/3:3:3)


**וְהַמַּלְוֶה בְרִבִּית.** אֶחָד הַלֹּוֶה וְאֶחָד הַמַּלְוֶה שְׁנֵיהֶם פְּסוּלִין, דְּקַיְמָא לָן הַמַּלְוֶה וְהַלֹּוֶה שְׁנֵיהֶם עוֹבְרִים בְּלֹא תַעֲשֶׂה:


ומלוה בריבית – both the borrower and the lender are ineligible, and we hold that both the lender and the borrower violate a negative commandment.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 3:3:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/3:3:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/3:3:4)


**וּמַפְרִיחֵי יוֹנִים.** אִית דִּמְפָרְשֵׁי מִין מִמִּינֵי הַשְּׂחוֹק, אִם תַּקְדִּים יוֹנָתְךָ לְיוֹנָתִי אֶתֵּן לְךָ כָּךְ וְכָךְ. וְאִית דִּמְפָרְשֵׁי שֶׁמְּגַדֵּל יוֹנָה מְלֻמֶּדֶת לְהָבִיא יוֹנִים לְבֵית בְּעָלֶיהָ בְּעַל כָּרְחָן. וְיֵשׁ בָּהֶן גָּזֵל מִפְּנֵי דַרְכֵי שָׁלוֹם, וְלֹא גָזֵל גָּמוּר:


ומפריחי יונים – there are those who explain this – as one of a kind of game/competition: “If yuour dove will come before my dove, I will give you such and such.” And there those who explain this as one who raises doves who know how to bring [other] doves to the house of its master against their will. And there is in this theft because of the ways of peace, but not complete theft.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 3:3:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/3:3:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/3:3:5)


**וְסוֹחֲרֵי שְׁבִיעִית.** עוֹשִׂין סְחוֹרָה בְּפֵרוֹת שְׁבִיעִית. וְהַתּוֹרָה אָמְרָה (ויקרא כה) וְהָיְתָה שַׁבַּת הָאָרֶץ לָכֶם לְאָכְלָה, וְלֹא לִסְחוֹרָה:


וסוחרי שביעית – who do business with seventh year produce, and the Torah stated: (Leviticus 25:6): “But you may eat whatever the land during its sabbath will produce...,” but not for business.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 3:3:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/3:3:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/3:3:6)


**רַבִּי שִׁמְעוֹן אוֹמֵר וְכוּ'.** בַּגְּמָרָא מְפָרֵשׁ מִלְּתֵיהּ דְּרַבִּי שִׁמְעוֹן הָכִי, בַּתְּחִלָּה הָיוּ קוֹרְאִים אוֹתָם אוֹסְפֵי שְׁבִיעִית, כְּלוֹמַר אוֹסְפֵי פֵרוֹת שְׁבִיעִית לְעַצְמָן הָיוּ פְסוּלִים לְעֵדוּת כְּמוֹ סוֹחֲרֵי פֵרוֹת שְׁבִיעִית:


רבי שמעון אומר: – In the Gemara, it explains this matter of Rabbi Shimon as such: At first, they would call them “people who collect seventh-year produce for themselves,” who were ineligible for testimony, just like those who engaged in the business of seventh-year produce.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Sanhedrin 3:3:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/3:3:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Sanhedrin/r/3:3:7)


**מִשֶּׁרַבּוּ הָאַנָּסִין.** הַשּׁוֹאֲלִין מְנַת הַמֶּלֶךְ כָּךְ וְכָךְ כּוֹרִין תְּבוּאָה בְכָל שָׁנָה, וְהָיוּ צְרִיכִין לֶאֱסֹף תְּבוּאָה שֶׁל שְׁבִיעִית לִפְרֹעַ מִמֶּנָּה מְנַת הַמֶּלֶךְ, חָזְרוּ לוֹמַר סוֹחֲרֵי פֵרוֹת שְׁבִיעִית בִּלְבָד הֵם פְּסוּלִים, אֲבָל אוֹסְפֵי תְבוּאַת שְׁבִיעִית לָתֵת לַמֶּלֶךְ כְּשֵׁרִים לְעֵדוּת, כֵּיוָן שֶׁאֵין אוֹסְפִים לֶאֱצֹר לְעַצְמָן. וּלְעִנְיַן פְּסַק הֲלָכָה, כָּל מִי שֶׁעָבַר עֲבֵרָה שֶׁחַיָּב עָלֶיהָ מִיתַת בֵּית דִּין אוֹ כָרֵת אוֹ מַלְקוֹת, פָּסוּל לְעֵדוּת. דִּמְחֻיָּב מִיתָה נִקְרָא רָשָׁע, דִּכְתִיב (במדבר לה) אֲשֶׁר הוּא רָשָׁע לָמוּת, וּבְחַיָּבֵי מַלְקוֹת כְּתִיב (דברים כה) וְהָיָה אִם בִּן הַכּוֹת הָרָשָׁע, וְהַתּוֹרָה אָמְרָה (שמות כג) אַל תָּשֶׁת יָדְךָ עִם רָשָׁע לִהְיֹת עֵד חָמָס, וְדָרְשִׁינַן אַל תָּשֶׁת רָשָׁע עֵד. וְאִם לָקָה חָזַר לְהֶכְשֵׁרוֹ, דִּכְתִיב וְנִקְלָה אָחִיךָ לְעֵינֶיךָ, כֵּיוָן שֶׁלָּקָה הֲרֵי הוּא כְאָחִיךָ. וְאִם לָקַח מָמוֹן שֶׁלֹּא כַדִּין, אַף עַל פִּי שֶׁאֵינוֹ חַיָּב בֵּיהּ לֹא מִיתָה וְלֹא מַלְקוֹת, פָּסוּל לְעֵדוּת, כְּגוֹן גַּנָּב וְגַזְלָן וּמַלְוֶה בְרִבִּית. וְאִם לָקַח מָמוֹן שֶׁיֵּשׁ בּוֹ אִסּוּר דְּרַבָּנָן, פָּסוּל לְעֵדוּת מִדְּרַבָּנָן, כְּגוֹן מַפְרִיחֵי יוֹנִים, וְחַמְסָן דְּיָהֵיב דְּמֵי וְלוֹקֵחַ הַחֵפֶץ שֶׁאֵין הַבְּעָלִים רוֹצִים לְמָכְרוֹ, וְהַגַּבָּאִים וְהַמּוֹכְסִים שֶׁלּוֹקְחִים לְעַצְמָן, וּמְקַבְּלֵי צְדָקָה מִן הַנָּכְרִים בְּפַרְהֶסְיָא, אֵלּוּ וְכַיּוֹצֵא בָהֶן פְּסוּלִין לְעֵדוּת מִדְּרַבָּנָן, וְאֵין עֵדוּתָן בְּטֵלָה עַד שֶׁיַּכְרִיזוּ עֲלֵיהֶם וִיפַרְסְמוּ אוֹתָן. אֲבָל פְּסוּלֵי עֵדוּת שֶׁל תּוֹרָה אֵין צְרִיכִין הַכְרָזָה. וְכָל הַפְּסוּלִין לְעֵדוּת, בֵּין שֶׁל תּוֹרָה בֵּין שֶׁל דִּבְרֵיהֶם, אִם נוֹדַע מֵעִנְיָנָם שֶׁעָשׂוּ תְשׁוּבָה גְמוּרָה וְהַמָּמוֹן שֶׁלָּקְחוּ שֶׁלֹּא כַדִּין הֶחֱזִירוּהוּ וְעָשׂוּ סְיָג וְגָדֵר לְעַצְמָן בְּאוֹתוֹ דָבָר שֶׁחָטְאוּ בוֹ שֶׁלֹּא יוֹסִיפוּ לַעֲשׂוֹתוֹ עוֹד, הֲרֵי אֵלּוּ חָזְרוּ לְהֶכְשֵׁרָן. וּמְשַׂחֵק בְּקֻבְיָא אַף עַל פִּי שֶׁאֵין בָּזֶה גָזֵל אֲפִלּוּ מִדִּבְרֵיהֶם, הֵם פְּסוּלִים לְעֵדוּת, לְפִי שֶׁאֵין מִתְעַסְּקִין בְּיִשּׁוּבוֹ שֶׁל עוֹלָם וְאֵין בָּהֶם יִרְאַת שָׁמַיִם. וְדַוְקָא שֶׁאֵין לָהֶם מְלָאכָה וְאֻמָּנוּת אֶלָּא הִיא, כְּדִבְרֵי רַבִּי יְהוּדָה, וְכֵן הֲלָכָה. וְאֵימָתַי חֲזָרָתָן, מִשֶּׁיְּשַׁבְּרוּ פַסְפַּסֵּיהֶן וִיקַבְּלוּ עֲלֵיהֶם דַּאֲפִלּוּ בְחִנָּם לֹא עָבְדֵי:


משרבו האנסין – those who request portions of the King – such and such Korim of grain in each year. And they needed to gather the grain of the seventh year to pay off from it the portions of the king. And they went back to call only those who do business with grain of the seventh year are ineligible [to serve testify] but those who collect grain of the seventh year to give to the king are valid for testimony, and since they don’t collect it to store for themselves. But with regard to Halakhic decision-making, whomever transgressed a sin which makes him liable for death by the Jewish court or extirpation or flogging, is ineligible for testimony. And those liable for death are called רשע/wicked, as it is written (Numbers 35:31): “[You shall not accept a ransom for the life of a person] who is guilty of a capital crime; [he must be put to death].” And regarding those who are liable for flogging, it is written (Deuteronomy 25:2): “And if the guilty one is to be flogged…” And the Torah stated (Exodus 23:10): “You shall not join hands with the guilty to act as a malicious witness.” And the Rabbis expound on “You shall not join guilty hands.” And if he is flogged, he returns to his validity [as a witness], as it is written (Deuteronomy 25:3): “Your brother be degraded before your eyes.” Since he was flogged, he is like your brother. And if he took money, not according to the law, even though he is not liable by it for death or flogging, he ineligible for testimony, such as a thief and a robber and one who lends at interest. But if he took monies which have a Rabbinical prohibition, he is Rabbinically ineligible for testimony, such as those who are dove-racers (i.e., pigeon-racers), and an extortioner who gave money and took an object that the owners did not want to sell, and the which the tax-collectors and the customs collectors , who took it for themselves, and those who receive tzedakah in public from the heathens, these and those like them are Rabbinically ineligible for testimony. And their testimony is not nullified until they announce about them and make them public knowledge. But those who are ineligible to testify from the Torah do not require announcement, and all who are ineligible to testify whether from the Torah or from their words (i.e., the Rabbis), if it is known from their affairs that they completely repented and the monies that they took illegally were returned and that they made a fence and a partition (i.e., a guard against transgressing the law) for themselves in that matter where they had sinned so that they would not add to do this any further; behold, these returned to the validity [for testimony]. But those who are dice players, even though there is now theft, even from their words (i.e., the Rabbis), they are ineligible for testimony, because they do not engage in the welfare of the world and lack the fear of heaven. And especially because they have no work or craft other than this, according to the words of Rabbi Yehuda. And this is the Halakha. And when is their return? When they break their dice and accept upon themselves that even for free they should not do this.
